class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Eric_Williams" title="Eric Williams">Eric Williams</a><br /><small><a href="/wiki/List_of_prime_ministers_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="List of prime ministers of Trinidad and Tobago">Prime Minister</a></small></div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Trinidad and Tobago gained its <a href="/wiki/Trinidad_and_Tobago_Independence_Act_1962" title="Trinidad and Tobago Independence Act 1962">independence</a> from the <a href="/wiki/United_Kingdom" title="United Kingdom">United Kingdom</a> on 31 August 1962.<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-WDL_62-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-WDL-62">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Elizabeth_II" title="Elizabeth II">Elizabeth II</a> remained head of state as <a href="/wiki/Queen_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Queen of Trinidad and Tobago">Queen of Trinidad and Tobago</a>, represented locally by <a href="/wiki/List_of_heads_of_state_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago#Governor-General" title="List of heads of state of Trinidad and Tobago">Governor-General</a> <a href="/wiki/Solomon_Hochoy" title="Solomon Hochoy">Solomon Hochoy</a>. Eric Williams of the <a href="/wiki/People%27s_National_Movement" title="People&#39;s National Movement">People's National Movement</a>, a noted historian and intellectual regarded by some as the "<a href="/wiki/Father_of_the_Nation" title="Father of the Nation">Father of The Nation</a>", became the first <a href="/wiki/List_of_Prime_Ministers_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Prime Ministers of Trinidad and Tobago">Prime Minister</a>, serving in that capacity uninterrupted until 1981.<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> The dominant figure in the opposition in the early independence years was <a href="/wiki/Leader_of_the_Opposition_(Trinidad_and_Tobago)" title="Leader of the Opposition (Trinidad and Tobago)">Opposition Leader</a> <a href="/wiki/Rudranath_Capildeo" title="Rudranath Capildeo">Rudranath Capildeo</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Labour_Party_(Trinidad_and_Tobago)" title="Democratic Labour Party (Trinidad and Tobago)">Democratic Labour Party</a>. The first <a href="/wiki/List_of_speakers_of_the_House_of_Representatives_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="List of speakers of the House of Representatives of Trinidad and Tobago">Speaker of the House of Representatives</a> was Clytus Arnold Thomasos and the first <a href="/wiki/President_of_the_Senate_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="President of the Senate of Trinidad and Tobago">President of the Senate</a> was J. Hamilton Maurice. The 1960s saw the rise of a <a href="/wiki/Black_Power_Revolution" title="Black Power Revolution">Black Power movement</a>, inspired in part by the <a href="/wiki/Civil_rights_movement" title="Civil rights movement">civil rights movement</a> in the United States. Protests and strikes became common, with events coming to head in April 1970 when police shot dead a protester named Basil Davis.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018256_65-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018256-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Fearing a breakdown of law and order, Prime Minister Williams declared a state of emergency and ordered that many of the Black Power leaders be arrested. Some army leaders who were sympathetic to the Black Power movement, notably <a href="/wiki/Raffique_Shah" title="Raffique Shah">Raffique Shah</a> and <a href="/wiki/Rex_Lassalle" title="Rex Lassalle">Rex Lassalle</a>, attempted to mutiny; however, this was quashed by the <a href="/wiki/Trinidad_and_Tobago_Coast_Guard" class="mw-redirect" title="Trinidad and Tobago Coast Guard">Trinidad and Tobago Coast Guard</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018256_65-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018256-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Williams and the PNM retained power, largely due to divisions in the opposition.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018256_65-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018256-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 1963 Tobago was struck by <a href="/wiki/Hurricane_Flora" title="Hurricane Flora">Hurricane Flora</a>, which killed 30 people and resulted in enormous destruction across the island.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018236_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018236-67">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> Partly as a result of this, tourism came to replace agriculture as the island's primary source of income in the subsequent decades.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018236_67-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018236-67">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup> On 1 May 1968, Trinidad and Tobago joined the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Free_Trade_Association" title="Caribbean Free Trade Association">Caribbean Free Trade Association</a> (CARIFTA), which provided a continued economic, rather than political, linkage between the former <a href="/wiki/British_West_Indies" title="British West Indies">British West Indies</a> <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_Caribbean" title="Commonwealth Caribbean">English-speaking countries</a> after the <a href="/wiki/West_Indies_Federation" title="West Indies Federation">West Indies Federation</a> failed. On 1 August 1973, the country became a founding member state of CARIFTA's successor, the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Community" title="Caribbean Community">Caribbean Community</a> (CARICOM), which is a <a href="/wiki/Political_union" title="Political union">political</a> and <a href="/wiki/Economic_union" title="Economic union">economic union</a> between several <a href="/wiki/Caribbean" title="Caribbean">Caribbean</a> countries and territories.
</p><p>Between the years 1972 and 1983, the country profited greatly from the rising <a href="/wiki/Price_of_oil" title="Price of oil">price of oil</a> and the discovery of vast new oil deposits in its territorial waters, resulting in an economic boom that substantially increased living standards.<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018256_65-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018256-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> In 1976 the country became a republic within the <a href="/wiki/Commonwealth_of_Nations" title="Commonwealth of Nations">Commonwealth</a>, though it retained the <a href="/wiki/Judicial_Committee_of_the_Privy_Council" title="Judicial Committee of the Privy Council">Judicial Committee of the Privy Council</a> as its final appellate court.<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> The position of governor-general was replaced with that of <a href="/wiki/President_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="President of Trinidad and Tobago">President</a>; <a href="/wiki/Ellis_Clarke" title="Ellis Clarke">Ellis Clarke</a> was the first to hold this largely ceremonial role.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> Tobago was granted limited self-rule with the creation of the <a href="/wiki/Tobago_House_of_Assembly" title="Tobago House of Assembly">Tobago House of Assembly</a> in 1980.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018239_54-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018239-54">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Williams died in 1981, being replaced by <a href="/wiki/George_Chambers" title="George Chambers">George Chambers</a> who led the country until 1986. By this time a fall in the price of oil had resulted in a recession, causing rising inflation and unemployment.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018257_69-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018257-69">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> The main opposition parties united under the banner of <a href="/wiki/National_Alliance_for_Reconstruction" title="National Alliance for Reconstruction">National Alliance for Reconstruction</a> (NAR) and won the <a href="/wiki/1986_Trinidad_and_Tobago_general_election" title="1986 Trinidad and Tobago general election">1986 Trinidad and Tobago general election</a>, with NAR leader <a href="/wiki/A._N._R._Robinson" title="A. N. R. Robinson">A. N. R. Robinson</a> becoming the new Prime Minister.<sup id="cite_ref-N1_70-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-N1-70">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018256_65-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018256-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> Robinson was unable to hold together the fragile NAR coalition, and his economic reforms, such as the implementation of an <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">International Monetary Fund</a> <a href="/wiki/Structural_Adjustment_Program" class="mw-redirect" title="Structural Adjustment Program">Structural Adjustment Program</a> and devaluation of currency led to social unrest.<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> In 1990, 114 members of the <a href="/wiki/Jamaat_al_Muslimeen" title="Jamaat al Muslimeen">Jamaat al Muslimeen</a>, led by <a href="/wiki/Yasin_Abu_Bakr" title="Yasin Abu Bakr">Yasin Abu Bakr</a> (formerly known as Lennox Phillip) stormed the <a href="/wiki/The_Red_House_(Trinidad_and_Tobago)" class="mw-redirect" title="The Red House (Trinidad and Tobago)">Red House</a> (the seat of <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Parliament of Trinidad and Tobago">Parliament</a>), and <a href="/wiki/Trinidad_and_Tobago_Television" title="Trinidad and Tobago Television">Trinidad and Tobago Television</a>, the only television station in the country at the time, <a href="/wiki/Jamaat_al_Muslimeen_coup_attempt" title="Jamaat al Muslimeen coup attempt">holding Robinson and country's government hostage</a> for six days before surrendering.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> The coup leaders were promised amnesty, but upon their surrender they were  arrested, ultimately being released after protracted legal wrangling.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018253_46-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018253-46">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The PNM under <a href="/wiki/Patrick_Manning" title="Patrick Manning">Patrick Manning</a> returned to power following the <a href="/wiki/1991_Trinidad_and_Tobago_general_election" title="1991 Trinidad and Tobago general election">1991 Trinidad and Tobago general election</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Hoping to capitalise on an improvement in the economy, Manning called <a href="/wiki/1995_Trinidad_and_Tobago_general_election" title="1995 Trinidad and Tobago general election">an early election</a> in 1995, however, this resulted in a <a href="/wiki/Hung_parliament" title="Hung parliament">hung parliament</a>. Two NAR representatives backed the opposition <a href="/wiki/United_National_Congress" title="United National Congress">United National Congress</a> (UNC), which had split off from the NAR in 1989, and they thus took power under <a href="/wiki/Basdeo_Panday" title="Basdeo Panday">Basdeo Panday</a>, who became the country's first Indo-Trinidadian Prime Minister.<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-19" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018257_69-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018257-69">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> After a period of political confusion caused by a series of inconclusive election results, Patrick Manning returned to power in 2001, retaining that position until 2010.<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2003 the country entered a second oil boom, and petroleum, petrochemicals and natural gas continue to be the backbone of the economy. Tourism and the public service are the mainstay of the economy of Tobago, though authorities have attempted to diversify the island's economy.<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> A corruption scandal resulted in Manning's defeat by the newly formed <a href="/wiki/People%27s_Partnership" title="People&#39;s Partnership">People's Partnership</a> coalition in 2010, with <a href="/wiki/Kamla_Persad-Bissessar" title="Kamla Persad-Bissessar">Kamla Persad-Bissessar</a> becoming the country's first female Prime Minister.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018258_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018258-74">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> However, corruption allegations bedevilled the new administration, and the PP were defeated in 2015 by the PNM under <a href="/wiki/Keith_Rowley" title="Keith Rowley">Keith Rowley</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018259_77-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018259-77">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> In August 2020, the governing People's National Movement won general <a href="/wiki/2020_Trinidad_and_Tobago_general_election" title="2020 Trinidad and Tobago general election">election</a>, earning the incumbent Prime Minister Keith Rowley a second term in office.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=11" title="Edit section: Geography">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Geography of Trinidad and Tobago">Geography of Trinidad and Tobago</a></div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1034237262">.mw-parser-output .stack{box-sizing:border-box}.mw-parser-output .stack>div{margin:1px;overflow:hidden}@media all and (min-width:720px){.mw-parser-output .stack-clear-left{float:left;clear:left}.mw-parser-output .stack-clear-right{float:right;clear:right}.mw-parser-output .stack-left{float:left}.mw-parser-output .stack-right{float:right}.mw-parser-output .stack-margin-clear-left{float:left;clear:left;margin-right:1em}.mw-parser-output .stack-margin-clear-right{float:right;clear:right;margin-left:1em}.mw-parser-output .stack-margin-left{float:left;margin-right:1em}.mw-parser-output .stack-margin-right{float:right;margin-left:1em}}</style><div class="stack stack-right"><div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:282px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Td-map.gif" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/01/Td-map.gif/280px-Td-map.gif" decoding="async" width="280" height="300" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/0/01/Td-map.gif 1.5x" data-file-width="342" data-file-height="367" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Td-map.gif" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>A map of Trinidad and Tobago</div></div></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:282px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Trinidad_Topography.png" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Trinidad_Topography.png/280px-Trinidad_Topography.png" decoding="async" width="280" height="273" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Trinidad_Topography.png/420px-Trinidad_Topography.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/8/83/Trinidad_Topography.png/560px-Trinidad_Topography.png 2x" data-file-width="1750" data-file-height="1704" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Trinidad_Topography.png" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Trinidad and Tobago's topography</div></div></div>
</div></div>
<p>Trinidad and Tobago is situated between 10° 2' and 11° 12' N <a href="/wiki/Latitude" title="Latitude">latitude</a> and 60° 30' and 61° 56' W <a href="/wiki/Longitude" title="Longitude">longitude</a>, with the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Sea" title="Caribbean Sea">Caribbean Sea</a> to the north, the <a href="/wiki/Atlantic_Ocean" title="Atlantic Ocean">Atlantic Ocean</a> to the east and south, and the <a href="/wiki/Gulf_of_Paria" title="Gulf of Paria">Gulf of Paria</a> to the west. It is located in the far south-east of the Caribbean region, with the island of Trinidad being just 11 kilometres (6.8&#160;mi) off the coast of <a href="/wiki/Venezuela" title="Venezuela">Venezuela</a> in mainland <a href="/wiki/South_America" title="South America">South America</a> across the <a href="/wiki/Columbus_Channel" title="Columbus Channel">Columbus Channel</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> The islands are a physiographical extension of <a href="/wiki/South_America" title="South America">South America</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_80-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-80">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> Covering an area of 5,128&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (1,980&#160;sq&#160;mi),<sup id="cite_ref-back_81-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-back-81">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> the country consists of two main islands, Trinidad and <a href="/wiki/Tobago" title="Tobago">Tobago</a>, separated by a 20-mile (32&#160;km) strait, plus a number of much <a href="/wiki/List_of_islands_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="List of islands of Trinidad and Tobago">smaller islands</a>, including <a href="/wiki/Chacachacare" title="Chacachacare">Chacachacare</a>, <a href="/wiki/Monos" title="Monos">Monos</a>, <a href="/wiki/Huevos_(island)" title="Huevos (island)">Huevos</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gaspar_Grande" title="Gaspar Grande">Gaspar Grande</a> (or Gasparee), <a href="/wiki/Little_Tobago" title="Little Tobago">Little Tobago</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Saint_Giles_Island" title="Saint Giles Island">Saint Giles Island</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Trinidad is 4,768&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (1,841&#160;sq&#160;mi) in area (comprising 93.0% of the country's total area) with an average length of 80 kilometres (50&#160;mi) and an average width of 59 kilometres (37&#160;mi). Tobago has an area of about 300&#160;km<sup>2</sup> (120&#160;sq&#160;mi), or 5.8% of the country's area, is 41&#160;km (25&#160;mi) long and 12&#160;km (7.5&#160;mi) at its greatest width. Trinidad and Tobago lie on the continental shelf of South America, and are thus geologically considered to lie entirely in South America.<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The terrain of the islands is a mixture of mountains and plains.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago_16-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> On Trinidad the <a href="/wiki/Northern_Range" title="Northern Range">Northern Range</a> runs parallel with the north coast, and contains the country's highest peak (<a href="/wiki/El_Cerro_del_Aripo" title="El Cerro del Aripo">El Cerro del Aripo</a>), which is 940 metres (3,080&#160;ft) above sea level,<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago_16-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> and second highest (<a href="/wiki/El_Tucuche" title="El Tucuche">El Tucuche</a>, 936 metres (3,071&#160;ft)).<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> The rest of the island is generally flatter, excluding the <a href="/wiki/Central_Range,_Trinidad_and_Tobago" class="mw-redirect" title="Central Range, Trinidad and Tobago">Central Range</a> and Montserrat Hills in the centre of the island and the <a href="/wiki/Southern_Range" title="Southern Range">Southern Range</a> and <a href="/wiki/Trinity_Hills" title="Trinity Hills">Trinity Hills</a> in the south. The three mountain ranges determine the drainage pattern of Trinidad.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica_80-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica-80">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> The east coast is noted for its beaches, most notably <a href="/wiki/Manzanilla_Beach,_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Manzanilla Beach, Trinidad and Tobago">Manzanilla Beach</a>. The island contains several large swamp areas, such as the <a href="/wiki/Caroni_Swamp" title="Caroni Swamp">Caroni Swamp</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Nariva_Swamp" title="Nariva Swamp">Nariva Swamp</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-25" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Major bodies of water on Trinidad include the <a href="/wiki/Hollis_Reservoir" title="Hollis Reservoir">Hollis Reservoir</a>, <a href="/wiki/Navet_Dam" title="Navet Dam">Navet Reservoir</a>, <a href="/wiki/Caroni%E2%80%93Arena_Dam" title="Caroni–Arena Dam">Caroni Reservoir</a>.  Trinidad is made up of a variety of soil types, the majority being fine sands and heavy clays. The alluvial valleys of the Northern Range and the soils of the <a href="/wiki/East%E2%80%93West_Corridor" title="East–West Corridor">East–West Corridor</a> are the most fertile.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2015)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Trinidad is also notable for containing <a href="/wiki/Pitch_Lake" title="Pitch Lake">Pitch Lake</a>, the largest natural reservoir of <a href="/wiki/Bitumen" title="Bitumen">asphalt</a> in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago_16-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-26" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> Tobago contains a flat plain in its south-west, with the eastern half of the island being more mountainous, culminating in Pigeon Peak, the island's highest point at 550 metres (1,800&#160;ft).<sup id="cite_ref-Anthony_83-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Anthony-83">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup> Tobago also contains several coral reefs off its coast.<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The majority of the population reside on the island of Trinidad, and this is thus the location of largest <a href="/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_in_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="List of cities and towns in Trinidad and Tobago">towns and cities</a>. There are four major municipalities in Trinidad: the capital Port of Spain, <a href="/wiki/San_Fernando,_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="San Fernando, Trinidad and Tobago">San Fernando</a>, <a href="/wiki/Arima" title="Arima">Arima</a> and <a href="/wiki/Chaguanas" title="Chaguanas">Chaguanas</a>. The main town on Tobago is <a href="/wiki/Scarborough,_Tobago" title="Scarborough, Tobago">Scarborough</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Geology">Geology</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=12" title="Edit section: Geology">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Trinidad#Geology" title="Trinidad">Trinidad §&#160;Geology</a></div>
<p>The Northern Range consists mainly of Upper Jurassic and <a href="/wiki/Cretaceous" title="Cretaceous">Cretaceous</a> <a href="/wiki/Metamorphic_rock" title="Metamorphic rock">metamorphic rocks</a>. The Northern Lowlands (the East–West Corridor and <a href="/wiki/Caroni_Plain" title="Caroni Plain">Caroni Plain</a>) consist of younger shallow marine <a href="/wiki/Clastic_sediment" class="mw-redirect" title="Clastic sediment">clastic sediments</a>. South of this, the <a href="/wiki/Central_Range,_Trinidad" class="mw-redirect" title="Central Range, Trinidad">Central Range</a> <a href="/wiki/Fold_and_thrust_belt" title="Fold and thrust belt">fold and thrust belt</a> consists of Cretaceous and <a href="/wiki/Eocene" title="Eocene">Eocene</a> <a href="/wiki/Sedimentary_rock" title="Sedimentary rock">sedimentary rocks</a>, with <a href="/wiki/Miocene" title="Miocene">Miocene</a> formations along the southern and eastern flanks. The <a href="/wiki/Naparima_Plain" title="Naparima Plain">Naparima Plain</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Nariva_Swamp" title="Nariva Swamp">Nariva Swamp</a> form the southern shoulder of this uplift.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The Southern Lowlands consist of Miocene and <a href="/wiki/Pliocene" title="Pliocene">Pliocene</a> sands, clays, and gravels. These overlie oil and natural gas deposits, especially north of the Los Bajos Fault. The Southern Range forms the third <a href="/wiki/Anticline" title="Anticline">anticlinal</a> uplift. The rocks consist of <a href="/wiki/Sandstone" title="Sandstone">sandstones</a>, <a href="/wiki/Shale" title="Shale">shales</a>, <a href="/wiki/Siltstone" title="Siltstone">siltstones</a> and clays formed in the Miocene and uplifted in the <a href="/wiki/Pleistocene" title="Pleistocene">Pleistocene</a>. <a href="/wiki/Tar_sands" class="mw-redirect" title="Tar sands">Oil sands</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mud_volcano" title="Mud volcano">mud volcanoes</a> are especially common in this area.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=13" title="Edit section: Climate">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:STAPP_114_La_Brea_Pitch_Lake.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/STAPP_114_La_Brea_Pitch_Lake.jpg/220px-STAPP_114_La_Brea_Pitch_Lake.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="125" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/STAPP_114_La_Brea_Pitch_Lake.jpg/330px-STAPP_114_La_Brea_Pitch_Lake.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d8/STAPP_114_La_Brea_Pitch_Lake.jpg/440px-STAPP_114_La_Brea_Pitch_Lake.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3840" data-file-height="2178" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:STAPP_114_La_Brea_Pitch_Lake.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Pitch Lake, in south-west Trinidad</div></div></div>
<p>Trinidad and Tobago has a maritime <a href="/wiki/Tropical_climate" title="Tropical climate">tropical climate</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago_16-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> There are two seasons annually: the <a href="/wiki/Dry_season" title="Dry season">dry season</a> for the first five months of the year, and the <a href="/wiki/Rainy_season" class="mw-redirect" title="Rainy season">rainy season</a> in the remaining seven of the year. Winds are predominantly from the northeast and are dominated by the northeast <a href="/wiki/Trade_wind" class="mw-redirect" title="Trade wind">trade winds</a>. Unlike many Caribbean islands Trinidad and Tobago lies outside the main hurricane alleys; nevertheless, the island of Tobago was struck by Hurricane Flora on 30 September 1963. In the Northern Range of Trinidad, the climate is often cooler than that of the sweltering heat of the plains below, due to constant cloud and mist cover, and heavy rains in the mountains.
</p><p>Record temperatures for Trinidad and Tobago are 39&#160;°C (102&#160;°F)<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> for the high in Port of Spain, and a low of 12&#160;°C (54&#160;°F).<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Biodiversity">Biodiversity</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=14" title="Edit section: Biodiversity">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Environment_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Environment of Trinidad and Tobago">Environment of Trinidad and Tobago</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Mayaro_Beach;_Trinidad_%26_Tobago.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/Mayaro_Beach%3B_Trinidad_%26_Tobago.jpg/220px-Mayaro_Beach%3B_Trinidad_%26_Tobago.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/Mayaro_Beach%3B_Trinidad_%26_Tobago.jpg/330px-Mayaro_Beach%3B_Trinidad_%26_Tobago.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c7/Mayaro_Beach%3B_Trinidad_%26_Tobago.jpg/440px-Mayaro_Beach%3B_Trinidad_%26_Tobago.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1600" data-file-height="1200" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Mayaro_Beach;_Trinidad_%26_Tobago.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div><a href="/wiki/Mayaro,_Trinidad" title="Mayaro, Trinidad">Mayaro</a> Beach, in the southeastern area of Trinidad</div></div></div>
<p>Because Trinidad and Tobago lies on the continental shelf of South America, and in ancient times were physically connected to the South American mainland, its biological diversity is unlike that of most other Caribbean islands, and has much more in common with that of Venezuela.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018270_86-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018270-86">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> The main ecosystems are: coastal and marine (coral reefs, mangrove swamps, open ocean and seagrass beds); forest; freshwater (rivers and streams); <a href="/wiki/Karst" title="Karst">karst</a>; man-made ecosystems (agricultural land, freshwater dams, secondary forest); and savanna. On 1 August 1996, Trinidad and Tobago ratified the 1992 Rio <a href="/wiki/Convention_on_Biological_Diversity" title="Convention on Biological Diversity">Convention on Biological Diversity</a>, and it has produced a biodiversity action plan and four reports describing the country's contribution to biodiversity conservation. These reports formally acknowledged the importance of biodiversity to the well-being of the country's people through provision of <a href="/wiki/Ecosystem_service" title="Ecosystem service">ecosystem services</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<div class="thumb tleft"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Leatherback_sea_turtle_Tinglar,_USVI_(5839996547).jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fc/Leatherback_sea_turtle_Tinglar%2C_USVI_%285839996547%29.jpg/220px-Leatherback_sea_turtle_Tinglar%2C_USVI_%285839996547%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="128" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fc/Leatherback_sea_turtle_Tinglar%2C_USVI_%285839996547%29.jpg/330px-Leatherback_sea_turtle_Tinglar%2C_USVI_%285839996547%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fc/Leatherback_sea_turtle_Tinglar%2C_USVI_%285839996547%29.jpg/440px-Leatherback_sea_turtle_Tinglar%2C_USVI_%285839996547%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1864" data-file-height="1085" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Leatherback_sea_turtle_Tinglar,_USVI_(5839996547).jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Trinidad and Tobago is a major nesting site for <a href="/wiki/Leatherback_sea_turtle" title="Leatherback sea turtle">Leatherback Turtles</a></div></div></div>
<p>Information about vertebrates is good, with 472 bird species (2 endemics), about 100 mammals, about 90 reptiles (a few endemics), about 30 amphibians (including several endemics), 50 freshwater fish and at least 950 marine fish.<sup id="cite_ref-Convention_on_Biological_Diversity_88-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Convention_on_Biological_Diversity-88">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> Notable mammal species include the <a href="/wiki/Ocelot" title="Ocelot">ocelot</a>, <a href="/wiki/West_Indian_manatee" title="West Indian manatee">West Indian manatee</a>, <a href="/wiki/Collared_peccary" title="Collared peccary">collared peccary</a> (known as the quenk locally), <a href="/wiki/Red-rumped_agouti" title="Red-rumped agouti">red-rumped agouti</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lowland_paca" title="Lowland paca">lappe</a>, <a href="/wiki/Red_brocket" title="Red brocket">red brocket deer</a>, <a href="/wiki/Neotropical_otter" title="Neotropical otter">Neotropical otter</a>, <a href="/wiki/Wedge-capped_capuchin" title="Wedge-capped capuchin">weeper capuchin</a> and <a href="/wiki/Guyanan_red_howler" title="Guyanan red howler">red howler monkey</a>; there are also some 70 species of <a href="/wiki/Bat" title="Bat">bat</a>, including the <a href="/wiki/Vampire_bat" title="Vampire bat">vampire bat</a> and <a href="/wiki/Fringe-lipped_bat" title="Fringe-lipped bat">fringe-lipped bat</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018273_89-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018273-89">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> The larger reptiles present include 5 species of marine turtles known to nest on the islands' beaches, the <a href="/wiki/Green_anaconda" title="Green anaconda">green anaconda</a>, the <i><a href="/wiki/Boa_constrictor" title="Boa constrictor">Boa constrictor</a></i> and the <a href="/wiki/Spectacled_caiman" title="Spectacled caiman">spectacled caiman</a>. There are at least 47 species of snakes, including only four dangerous venomous species (only in Trinidad and not in Tobago), lizards such as the <a href="/wiki/Green_iguana" title="Green iguana">green iguana</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Tupinambis_cryptus" title="Tupinambis cryptus">Tupinambis cryptus</a> and a few species of fresh water turtles and land tortoises.<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018274_90-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018274-90">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup> are present. Of the amphibians, the <a href="/wiki/Common_tree_frog" title="Common tree frog">golden tree frog</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mannophryne_trinitatis" title="Mannophryne trinitatis">Trinidad poison frog</a> are found in the highest peaks of Trinidad's Northern Range and nearby on <a href="/wiki/Venezuela" title="Venezuela">Venezuela</a>'s Paria Peninsula.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018274_90-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018274-90">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup> Marine life is abundant, with several species of <a href="/wiki/Sea_urchin" title="Sea urchin">sea urchin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Coral" title="Coral">coral</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lobster" title="Lobster">lobster</a>, <a href="/wiki/Anemone" title="Anemone">anemone</a>, <a href="/wiki/Starfish" title="Starfish">starfish</a>, <a href="/wiki/Manta_ray" title="Manta ray">manta ray</a>, <a href="/wiki/Dolphin" title="Dolphin">dolphin</a>, <a href="/wiki/Porpoise" title="Porpoise">porpoise</a> and <a href="/wiki/Whale_shark" title="Whale shark">whale shark</a> present in the islands' waters.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018276_92-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018276-92">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> The introduced <a href="/wiki/Pterois" title="Pterois">Pterois</a> is viewed as a pest, as it eats many native species of fish and has no natural predators; efforts are currently underway to cull the numbers of this species.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018276_92-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018276-92">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup> The country contains five terrestrial ecoregions: <a href="/wiki/Trinidad_and_Tobago_moist_forests" title="Trinidad and Tobago moist forests">Trinidad and Tobago moist forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lesser_Antillean_dry_forests" title="Lesser Antillean dry forests">Lesser Antillean dry forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Trinidad_and_Tobago_dry_forests" title="Trinidad and Tobago dry forests">Trinidad and Tobago dry forests</a>, <a href="/wiki/Windward_Islands_xeric_scrub" title="Windward Islands xeric scrub">Windward Islands xeric scrub</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Trinidad_mangroves" title="Trinidad mangroves">Trinidad mangroves</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_93-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-93">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Trinidad and Tobago is noted particularly for its large number of bird species, and is a popular destination for <a href="/wiki/Birdwatching" title="Birdwatching">bird watchers</a>. Notable species include the <a href="/wiki/Scarlet_ibis" title="Scarlet ibis">scarlet ibis</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rufous-vented_chachalaca" title="Rufous-vented chachalaca">cocrico</a>, <a href="/wiki/Egret" title="Egret">egret</a>, <a href="/wiki/Shiny_cowbird" title="Shiny cowbird">shiny cowbird</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bananaquit" title="Bananaquit">bananaquit</a>, <a href="/wiki/Oilbird" title="Oilbird">oilbird</a> and various species of <a href="/wiki/Honeycreeper" title="Honeycreeper">honeycreeper</a>, <a href="/wiki/Trogon" title="Trogon">trogon</a>, <a href="/wiki/Toucan" title="Toucan">toucan</a>, <a href="/wiki/Parrot" title="Parrot">parrot</a>, <a href="/wiki/Tanager" title="Tanager">tanager</a>, <a href="/wiki/Woodpecker" title="Woodpecker">woodpecker</a>, <a href="/wiki/Antbird" title="Antbird">antbird</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kite_(bird)" title="Kite (bird)">kites</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hawk" title="Hawk">hawks</a>, <a href="/wiki/Booby" title="Booby">boobies</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pelican" title="Pelican">pelicans</a> and <a href="/wiki/Vulture" title="Vulture">vultures</a>; there are also 17 species of hummingbird, including the <a href="/wiki/Tufted_coquette" title="Tufted coquette">tufted coquette</a> which is the world's third smallest.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018274–275_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018274–275-94">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Information about invertebrates is dispersed and very incomplete. About 650 butterflies,<sup id="cite_ref-Convention_on_Biological_Diversity_88-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Convention_on_Biological_Diversity-88">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> at least 672 beetles (from Tobago alone)<sup id="cite_ref-95" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-95">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> and 40 corals<sup id="cite_ref-Convention_on_Biological_Diversity_88-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Convention_on_Biological_Diversity-88">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> have been recorded.<sup id="cite_ref-Convention_on_Biological_Diversity_88-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Convention_on_Biological_Diversity-88">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> Other notable invertebrates include the <a href="/wiki/Cockroach" title="Cockroach">cockroach</a>, <a href="/wiki/Leafcutter_ant" title="Leafcutter ant">leaf-cutter ant</a> and numerous species of <a href="/wiki/Mosquito" title="Mosquito">mosquitoes</a>, <a href="/wiki/Termite" title="Termite">termites</a>, <a href="/wiki/Spider" title="Spider">spiders</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tarantula" title="Tarantula">tarantulas</a>.
</p><p>Although the list is far from complete, 1,647 species of fungi, including lichens, have been recorded.<sup id="cite_ref-96" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-96">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-98" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-98">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> The true total number of fungi is likely to be far higher, given the generally accepted estimate that only about 7% of all fungi worldwide have so far been discovered.<sup id="cite_ref-99" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-99">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup> A first effort to estimate the number of endemic fungi tentatively listed 407 species.<sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Information about micro-organisms is dispersed and very incomplete. Nearly 200 species of marine algae have been recorded.<sup id="cite_ref-Convention_on_Biological_Diversity_88-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Convention_on_Biological_Diversity-88">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> The true total number of micro-organism species must be much higher.
</p><p>Thanks to a recently published checklist, plant diversity in Trinidad and Tobago is well documented with about 3,300 species (59 endemic) recorded.<sup id="cite_ref-Convention_on_Biological_Diversity_88-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Convention_on_Biological_Diversity-88">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup> Despite significant felling, forests still cover about 40% of the country, and there are about 350 different species of tree.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018270_86-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018270-86">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> A notable tree is the <a href="/wiki/Manchineel" title="Manchineel">manchineel</a> which is extremely poisonous to humans, and even just touching its sap can cause severe blistering of the skin; the tree is often covered with warning signs. The country had a 2019 <a href="/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index" title="Forest Landscape Integrity Index">Forest Landscape Integrity Index</a> mean score of 6.62/10, ranking it 69th globally out of 172 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FLII-Supplementary_101-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-101">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Threats to the country's biodiversity include over-hunting and poaching (see <a href="/wiki/Hunting#Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Hunting">Hunting#Trinidad and Tobago</a>), habitat loss and fragmentation (particularly due to forest fires and land clearance for quarrying, agriculture, squatting, housing and industrial development and road construction), water pollution, and introduction of invasive species and pathogens.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government_and_politics">Government and politics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=15" title="Edit section: Government and politics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Politics of Trinidad and Tobago">Politics of Trinidad and Tobago</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"/><div class="thumb tmulti tleft"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:212px;max-width:212px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:102px;max-width:102px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:132px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:Paula-Mae_Weekes_2019.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/Paula-Mae_Weekes_2019.jpg/100px-Paula-Mae_Weekes_2019.jpg" decoding="async" width="100" height="133" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/Paula-Mae_Weekes_2019.jpg/150px-Paula-Mae_Weekes_2019.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/a/ac/Paula-Mae_Weekes_2019.jpg/200px-Paula-Mae_Weekes_2019.jpg 2x" data-file-width="463" data-file-height="617" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Paula-Mae_Weekes" title="Paula-Mae Weekes">Paula-Mae Weekes</a><br /><small><a href="/wiki/President_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="President of Trinidad and Tobago">President</a><br />since 19 March 2018</small></div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:106px;max-width:106px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:132px;overflow:hidden"><a href="/wiki/File:KeithRowley.jpg" class="image"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/63/KeithRowley.jpg/104px-KeithRowley.jpg" decoding="async" width="104" height="133" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/63/KeithRowley.jpg/156px-KeithRowley.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/63/KeithRowley.jpg/208px-KeithRowley.jpg 2x" data-file-width="706" data-file-height="900" /></a></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Keith_Rowley" title="Keith Rowley">Keith Rowley</a><br /><small><a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" class="mw-redirect" title="Prime Minister of Trinidad and Tobago">Prime Minister</a><br />since 9 September 2015</small></div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Trinidad and Tobago is a republic with a <a href="/wiki/Two-party_system" title="Two-party system">two-party system</a> and a <a href="/wiki/Bicameralism" title="Bicameralism">bicameral</a> parliamentary system based on the <a href="/wiki/Westminster_System" class="mw-redirect" title="Westminster System">Westminster System</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago_16-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a> of Trinidad and Tobago is the President, currently <a href="/wiki/Paula-Mae_Weekes" title="Paula-Mae Weekes">Paula-Mae Weekes</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago_16-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> This largely ceremonial role replaced that of the Governor-General (representing the <a href="/wiki/Queen_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Queen of Trinidad and Tobago">Monarch of Trinidad and Tobago</a>) upon Trinidad and Tobago's becoming a republic in 1976.<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-31" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a> is the <a href="/wiki/List_of_Prime_Ministers_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" class="mw-redirect" title="List of Prime Ministers of Trinidad and Tobago">Prime Minister</a>, currently <a href="/wiki/Keith_Rowley" title="Keith Rowley">Keith Rowley</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago_16-7" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> The President is elected by an <a href="/wiki/Electoral_college" title="Electoral college">Electoral college</a> consisting of the full membership of both houses of <a href="/wiki/Parliament_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Parliament of Trinidad and Tobago">Parliament</a>.
</p><p>Following a general election, which takes place every five years, the President appoints as Prime Minister the person who has the support of a majority in the House of Representatives; this has generally been the leader of the party which won the most seats in the election (except in the case of the <a href="/wiki/Trinidad_and_Tobago_general_election,_2001" class="mw-redirect" title="Trinidad and Tobago general election, 2001">2001 General Elections</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-32" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since 1980 Tobago has also had its own elections, separate from the general elections. In these elections, members are elected and serve in the unicameral Tobago House of Assembly.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago_16-8" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Parliament consists of the <a href="/wiki/Senate_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" class="mw-redirect" title="Senate of Trinidad and Tobago">Senate</a> (31 seats) and the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Representatives_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" class="mw-redirect" title="House of Representatives of Trinidad and Tobago">House of Representatives</a> (41 seats, plus the Speaker).<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago_16-9" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> The members of the Senate are appointed by the president; 16 Government Senators are appointed on the advice of the Prime Minister, six Opposition Senators are appointed on the advice of the <a href="/wiki/Leader_of_the_Opposition_(Trinidad_and_Tobago)" title="Leader of the Opposition (Trinidad and Tobago)">Leader of the Opposition</a>, currently <a href="/wiki/Kamla_Persad-Bissessar" title="Kamla Persad-Bissessar">Kamla Persad-Bissessar</a>, and nine Independent Senators are appointed by the President to represent other sectors of civil society. The 41 members of the House of Representatives are elected by the people for a maximum term of five years in a "<a href="/wiki/Plurality_voting_system" class="mw-redirect" title="Plurality voting system">first past the post</a>" system.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=16" title="Edit section: Administrative divisions">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Regions_and_municipalities_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Regions and municipalities of Trinidad and Tobago">Regions and municipalities of Trinidad and Tobago</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:302px;"><a href="/wiki/File:Regional_corporations_and_municipalities_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago.svg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Regional_corporations_and_municipalities_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago.svg/300px-Regional_corporations_and_municipalities_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago.svg.png" decoding="async" width="300" height="281" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Regional_corporations_and_municipalities_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago.svg/450px-Regional_corporations_and_municipalities_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f0/Regional_corporations_and_municipalities_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago.svg/600px-Regional_corporations_and_municipalities_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1000" data-file-height="938" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:Regional_corporations_and_municipalities_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago.svg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Regional corporations and municipalities of Trinidad and Tobago</div></div></div>
<p>Trinidad is split into 14 <a href="/wiki/Regions_and_municipalities_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Regions and municipalities of Trinidad and Tobago">regions and municipalities</a>, consisting of nine regions and five municipalities, which have a limited level of autonomy.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago_16-10" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup> The various councils are made up of a mixture of elected and appointed members. Elections are held every three years.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> Tobago is administered by the <a href="/wiki/Tobago_House_of_Assembly" title="Tobago House of Assembly">Tobago House of Assembly</a>. The country was formerly divided into <a href="/wiki/Counties_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Counties of Trinidad and Tobago">counties</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Political_culture">Political culture</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=17" title="Edit section: Political culture">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<p>The two main parties are the People's National Movement (PNM) and the United National Congress (UNC). Support for these parties appears to fall along ethnic lines, with the PNM consistently obtaining a majority of Afro-Trinidadian vote, and the UNC gaining a majority of Indo-Trinidadian support. Several smaller parties also exist. As of the August 2020 General Elections, there were 19 registered political parties. These include, the Progressive Empowerment Party, Trinidad Humanity Campaign, New National Vision, Movement for Social Justice, Congress of the People, Movement for National Development, <a href="/wiki/Progressive_Democratic_Patriots" title="Progressive Democratic Patriots">Progressive Democratic Patriots</a>, National Coalition for Transformation, Progressive Party, Independent Liberal Party, Democratic Party of Trinidad and Tobago, National Organisation of We the People, Unrepresented Peoples Party, Trinidad and Tobago Democratic Front, The National Party, One Tobago Voice, and Unity of the Peoples.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=18" title="Edit section: Military">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Trinidad_and_Tobago_Defence_Force" title="Trinidad and Tobago Defence Force">Trinidad and Tobago Defence Force</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:222px;"><a href="/wiki/File:T%26T_Port_of_Spain-1-tonal.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/T%26T_Port_of_Spain-1-tonal.jpg/220px-T%26T_Port_of_Spain-1-tonal.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="145" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/T%26T_Port_of_Spain-1-tonal.jpg/330px-T%26T_Port_of_Spain-1-tonal.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/T%26T_Port_of_Spain-1-tonal.jpg/440px-T%26T_Port_of_Spain-1-tonal.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1996" data-file-height="1315" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:T%26T_Port_of_Spain-1-tonal.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>Coast Guard vessel in action</div></div></div>
<p>The Trinidad and Tobago Defence Force (TTDF) is the military organisation responsible for the defence of the twin island Republic of Trinidad and Tobago.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago_16-11" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> It consists of the <a href="/wiki/Trinidad_and_Tobago_Regiment" title="Trinidad and Tobago Regiment">Regiment</a>, the Coast Guard, the <a href="/wiki/Air_Force_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" class="mw-redirect" title="Air Force of Trinidad and Tobago">Air Guard</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Trinidad_and_Tobago_Defence_Force#Defence_Force_Reserves" title="Trinidad and Tobago Defence Force">Defence Force Reserves</a>. Established in 1962 after Trinidad and Tobago's independence from the United Kingdom, the TTDF is one of the largest military forces in the Anglophone Caribbean.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2011)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>Its mission statement is to "defend the sovereign good of The Republic of Trinidad and Tobago, contribute to the development of the national community and support the State in the fulfilment of its national and international objectives". The Defence Force has been engaged in domestic incidents, such as the <a href="/wiki/Jamaat_al_Muslimeen_coup_attempt" title="Jamaat al Muslimeen coup attempt">Jamaat al Muslimeen coup attempt</a>, and international missions, such as the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_Mission_in_Haiti" title="United Nations Mission in Haiti">United Nations Mission in Haiti</a> between 1993 and 1996.
</p><p>In 2019, Trinidad and Tobago signed the UN treaty on the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_on_the_Prohibition_of_Nuclear_Weapons" title="Treaty on the Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons">Prohibition of Nuclear Weapons</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=19" title="Edit section: Foreign relations">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Foreign relations of Trinidad and Tobago">Foreign relations of Trinidad and Tobago</a></div>
<p>Trinidad and Tobago maintains close relations with its <a href="/wiki/Caribbean" title="Caribbean">Caribbean</a> neighbours and major North American and European trading partners. As the most industrialised and second-largest country in the Anglophone Caribbean, Trinidad and Tobago has taken a leading role in the <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Community" title="Caribbean Community">Caribbean Community</a> (CARICOM), and strongly supports CARICOM economic integration efforts. It also is active in the <a href="/wiki/Summit_of_the_Americas" title="Summit of the Americas">Summit of the Americas</a> process and supports the establishment of the <a href="/wiki/Free_Trade_Area_of_the_Americas" title="Free Trade Area of the Americas">Free Trade Area of the Americas</a>, lobbying other nations for seating the Secretariat in Port of Spain.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>As a member of CARICOM, Trinidad and Tobago strongly backed efforts by the United States to bring political stability to <a href="/wiki/Haiti" title="Haiti">Haiti</a>, contributing personnel to the Multinational Force in 1994. After its 1962 independence, Trinidad and Tobago joined the United Nations and Commonwealth of Nations. In 1967 it became the first Commonwealth country to join the <a href="/wiki/Organization_of_American_States" title="Organization of American States">Organization of American States</a> (OAS).<sup id="cite_ref-oas.org_106-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-oas.org-106">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> In 1995 Trinidad played host to the inaugural meeting of the <a href="/wiki/Association_of_Caribbean_States" title="Association of Caribbean States">Association of Caribbean States</a> and has become the seat of this 35-member grouping, which seeks to further economic progress and integration among its states. In international forums, Trinidad and Tobago has defined itself as having an independent voting record, but often supports US and EU positions.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (August 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Law_enforcement_and_crime">Law enforcement and crime</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=20" title="Edit section: Law enforcement and crime">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Crime_in_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Crime in Trinidad and Tobago">Crime in Trinidad and Tobago</a></div>
<p>Trinidad and Tobago has in recent decades suffered from a relatively high crime rate;<sup id="cite_ref-smartraveller1_107-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-smartraveller1-107">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated1_108-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1-108">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> there are currently roughly 500 murders per year.<sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018256_65-6" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018256-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup> The country is a noted transshipment centre for the trafficking of illegal drugs from South America to the rest of the Caribbean and beyond to North America.<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup> Some estimates put the size of the "hidden economy" as high as 20–30% of measured GDP.<sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Terrorism">Terrorism</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=21" title="Edit section: Terrorism">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>Though there have been no terrorism-related incidents in the country since the <a href="/wiki/Jamaat_al_Muslimeen_coup_attempt" title="Jamaat al Muslimeen coup attempt">Jamaat al Muslimeen coup attempt</a>, Trinidad and Tobago remains a potential target; for example, in February 2018 a plot to attack the Carnival was foiled by police.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated1_108-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1-108">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> It is estimated that roughly 100 citizens of the country have travelled to the Middle East to fight for the <a href="/wiki/Islamic_State_of_Iraq_and_the_Levant" class="mw-redirect" title="Islamic State of Iraq and the Levant">Islamic State</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-smartraveller1_107-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-smartraveller1-107">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated1_108-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1-108">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup> In 2017 the government adopted a counter-terrorism and extremism strategy.<sup id="cite_ref-autogenerated1_108-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-autogenerated1-108">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Trinidad_and_Tobago_Prison_Service">Trinidad and Tobago Prison Service</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=22" title="Edit section: Trinidad and Tobago Prison Service">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The country's prison administration is the Trinidad and Tobago Prison Service (TTPS), it is under the control of the Commissioner of Prisons (Ag.) Dennis Pulchan, located in Port-of-Spain.<sup id="cite_ref-prisonstudies.org_112-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-prisonstudies.org-112">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> The prison population rate is 292 people per 100,000. The total prison population, including pre-trial detainees and remand prisoners, is 3,999 prisoners. The population rate of pre-trial detainees and remand prisoners is 174 per 100,000 of the national population (59.7% of the prison population). In 2018, the female prison population rate is 8.5 per 100,000 of the national population (2.9% of the prison population). Prisoners that are minors makes up 1.9% of the prison population and foreigners prisoners make 0.8% of the prison population. The occupancy level of Trinidad and Tobago's prison system is at 81.8% capacity.<sup id="cite_ref-prisonstudies.org_112-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-prisonstudies.org-112">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup> Trinidad and Tobago has nine prison establishments; Golden Grove Prison, Maximum Security Prison, Port of Spain Prison, Eastern Correctional Rehabilitation Centre, Remand Prison, Tobago Convict Prison, Carrera Convict Island Prison, Women's Prison and Youth Training and Rehabilitation Centre.<sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup> Trinidad and Tobago also use labour yards as prisons, or means of punishment.<sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=23" title="Edit section: Demographics">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Demographics of Trinidad and Tobago">Demographics of Trinidad and Tobago</a></div>
<p>The population of the country currently stands at 1,367,558 (June 2021 est.).<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (December 2019)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Ethnic_groups">Ethnic groups</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=24" title="Edit section: Ethnic groups">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago#Ethnic_groups" title="Demographics of Trinidad and Tobago">Demographics of Trinidad and Tobago §&#160;Ethnic groups</a></div>
