<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 0.4em; padding-right: 0.4em; min-width: 8em;">Other</td>
<td style="width: 100px; border-left: solid 1px silver; border-right: solid 1px silver;"><div style="background:Black; width: 1.3%; overflow: hidden;">&#8201;</div></td>
<td colspan="2" style="padding-left: 1.2em; padding-right: 0.4em; text-align: right;"> 1.3%</td>
</tr>

</tbody></table>
</div>
</div>
<p>The ethnic composition of Trinidad and Tobago reflects a history of conquest and immigration.<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup> While the earliest inhabitants were of Indigenous heritage, the two dominant groups in the country are now those of South Asian and of African heritage. <a href="/wiki/Indo-Trinidadian_and_Tobagonian" title="Indo-Trinidadian and Tobagonian">Indo-Trinidadian and Tobagonians</a> make up the country's largest ethnic group (approximately 35.4%);<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago_16-12" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> they are primarily the descendants of indentured workers from <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup> brought to replace freed African slaves who refused to continue working on the sugar plantations. Through cultural preservation many residents of Indian descent continue to maintain traditions from their ancestral homeland. Indo-Trinidadians reside primarily on Trinidad; as of the 2011 census only 2.5% of Tobago's population was of Indian descent.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_118-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-118">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Afro-Trinidadians_and_Tobagonians" title="Afro-Trinidadians and Tobagonians">Afro-Trinidadians and Tobagonians</a> make up the country's second largest ethnic group, with approximately 34.2% of the population identifying as being of African descent.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago_16-13" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> The majority of people of an African background are the descendants of slaves forcibly transported to the islands from as early as the 16th century. This group constitute the majority on Tobago, at 85.2%.<sup id="cite_ref-:2_118-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:2-118">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The bulk of the rest of the population are those who identify as being of mixed heritage.<sup id="cite_ref-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago_16-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-CIA_World_Factbook_-_Trinidad_and_Tobago-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> There are also small but significant minorities of people of <a href="/wiki/Indigenous_peoples_of_the_Americas" title="Indigenous peoples of the Americas">Indigenous</a>, <a href="/wiki/White_Trinidadian" class="mw-redirect" title="White Trinidadian">European</a>, <a href="/wiki/Portuguese_Trinidadian_and_Tobagonian" title="Portuguese Trinidadian and Tobagonian">Portuguese</a>, <a href="/wiki/Latino_(demonym)" title="Latino (demonym)">Latino</a>-<a href="/wiki/Hispanic" title="Hispanic">Hispanic</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chinese_Trinidadian_and_Tobagonian" class="mw-redirect" title="Chinese Trinidadian and Tobagonian">Chinese</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Arab" class="mw-redirect" title="Arab">Arab</a> descent.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Arima" title="Arima">Arima</a> in Trinidad is a noted centre of First Peoples' culture, including as the headquarters of the <a href="/wiki/Carib_Queen" title="Carib Queen">Carib Queen</a> and the location of the <a href="/wiki/Santa_Rosa_First_Peoples_Community" title="Santa Rosa First Peoples Community">Santa Rosa First Peoples Community</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-EBTT_11-35" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EBTT-11">&#91;10&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=25" title="Edit section: Languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago#Language" title="Demographics of Trinidad and Tobago">Demographics of Trinidad and Tobago §&#160;Language</a></div>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="English_and_English_creoles">English and English creoles</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=26" title="Edit section: English and English creoles">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Trinidadian_and_Tobagonian_English" title="Trinidadian and Tobagonian English">Trinidadian and Tobagonian English</a>, <a href="/wiki/Trinidadian_Creole" title="Trinidadian Creole">Trinidadian Creole</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Tobagonian_Creole" title="Tobagonian Creole">Tobagonian Creole</a></div>
<p>English is the country's official language (the local variety of standard English is <a href="/wiki/Trinidadian_and_Tobagonian_English" title="Trinidadian and Tobagonian English">Trinidadian and Tobagonian English</a> or more properly, Trinidad and Tobago Standard English, abbreviated as "TTSE"), but the main spoken language is either of two <a href="/wiki/English-based_creole_languages" title="English-based creole languages">English-based creole languages</a> (<a href="/wiki/Trinidadian_Creole" title="Trinidadian Creole">Trinidadian Creole</a> or <a href="/wiki/Tobagonian_Creole" title="Tobagonian Creole">Tobagonian Creole</a>), which reflects the Indigenous, European, African, and Asian heritage of the nation. Both creoles contain elements from a variety of African languages; Trinidadian English Creole, however, is also influenced by French and <a href="/wiki/Antillean_Creole_French" class="mw-redirect" title="Antillean Creole French">French Creole</a> (Patois).<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Hindustani">Hindustani</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=27" title="Edit section: Hindustani">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Caribbean_Hindustani#Trinidadian_Hindustani" title="Caribbean Hindustani">Caribbean Hindustani §&#160;Trinidadian Hindustani</a></div>
<p>The variant that is spoken in Trinidad and Tobago is known as <i>Trinidadian Hindustani</i>, <i>Trinidadian Bhojpuri</i>, <i>Trinidadian Hindi</i>, <i>Indian</i>, <i>Plantation Hindustani</i>, or <i>Gaon ke Bolee (Village Speech)</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_52-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-52">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> A majority of the early <a href="/wiki/Indo-Trinidadian_and_Tobagonian" title="Indo-Trinidadian and Tobagonian">Indian</a> indentured  immigrants spoke the <a href="/wiki/Bhojpuri" class="mw-redirect" title="Bhojpuri">Bhojpuri</a> and <a href="/wiki/Awadhi" class="mw-redirect" title="Awadhi">Awadhi</a> dialects, which later formed into Trinidadian Hindustani. In 1935, Indian movies began showing to audiences in Trinidad. Most of the Indian movies were in the <a href="/wiki/Hindustani_language" title="Hindustani language">Standard Hindustani (Hindi-Urdu) dialect</a> and this modified Trinidadian Hindustani slightly by adding Standard <a href="/wiki/Hindi" title="Hindi">Hindi</a> and <a href="/wiki/Urdu" title="Urdu">Urdu</a> phrases and vocabulary to Trinidadian Hindustani. Indian movies also revitalized Hindustani among Indo-Trinidadian and Tobagonians.<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> The British colonial government and estate owners had disdain and contempt for Hindustani and Indian languages in Trinidad. Due to this, many Indians saw it as a broken language keeping them in poverty and bound to the cane fields, and did not pass it on as a <a href="/wiki/First_language" title="First language">first language</a>, but rather as a <a href="/wiki/Heritage_language" title="Heritage language">heritage language</a>, as they favored English as a way out.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> Around the mid to late 1960s the <i>lingua franca</i> of Indo-Trinidadian and Tobagonians switched from Trinidadian Hindustani to a sort of <i>Hindinized</i> version of English. Today Hindustani survives on through <a href="/wiki/Indo-Caribbean_music" title="Indo-Caribbean music">Indo-Trinidadian and Tobagonian musical forms</a> such as, <a href="/wiki/Bhajan" title="Bhajan">Bhajan</a>, Indian classical music, Indian folk music, <a href="/wiki/Filmi" title="Filmi">Filmi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Pichakaree" title="Pichakaree">Pichakaree</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chutney_music" title="Chutney music">Chutney</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chutney_soca" title="Chutney soca">Chutney soca</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Chutney_parang" title="Chutney parang">Chutney parang</a>. As of 2003, there are about 15,633 Indo-Trinidadian and Tobagonians who speak Trinidadian Hindustani and as of 2011, there are about 10,000 who speak Standard Hindi. Many Indo-Trinidadians and Tobagonians today speak a type of <a href="/wiki/Hinglish" title="Hinglish">Hinglish</a> that consists of Trinidadian and Tobagonian English that is heavily laced with Trinidadian Hindustani vocabulary and phrases and many Indo-Trinidadians and Tobagonians can recite phrases or prayers in Hindustani today. There are many places in Trinidad and Tobago that have names of Hindustani origin. Some phrases and vocabulary have even made their way into the mainstream English and English Creole dialect of the country.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-auto_52-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-52">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-auto2_126-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto2-126">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/World_Hindi_Conference" title="World Hindi Conference">World Hindi Day</a> is celebrated each year on 10 January with events organized by the National Council of Indian Culture, Hindi Nidhi Foundation, Indian High Commission, Mahatma Gandhi Institute for Cultural Co-operation, and the <a href="/wiki/Sanatan_Dharma_Maha_Sabha" title="Sanatan Dharma Maha Sabha">Sanatan Dharma Maha Sabha</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Spanish">Spanish</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=28" title="Edit section: Spanish">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Spanish_language_in_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Spanish language in Trinidad and Tobago">Spanish language in Trinidad and Tobago</a></div>
<div class="excerpt-block"><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1066933788">.mw-parser-output .excerpt-hat .mw-editsection-like{font-style:normal}</style><div class="excerpt">
In 2014, <a href="/wiki/Spanish_language" title="Spanish language">Spanish</a> was the native language of around 4,000 people (or 0.3% of the total population) living in Trinidad and Tobago.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> The number has grown substantially thanks to the massive immigration of Venezuelans due to the <a href="/wiki/Crisis_in_Venezuela" title="Crisis in Venezuela">ongoing crisis</a> in the country.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (March 2020)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup></div></div>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Tamil">Tamil</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=29" title="Edit section: Tamil">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Tamil_language" title="Tamil language">Tamil language</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tamil_diaspora#Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Tamil diaspora">Tamil diaspora §&#160;Trinidad and Tobago</a></div>
<p>The Tamil language is spoken by some of the older <a href="/wiki/Tamils" title="Tamils">Tamil (Madrasi)</a> Indo-Trinidadian and Tobagonian population. It is mostly spoken by the few remaining children of indentured Indian labourers from the present-day state of <a href="/wiki/Tamil_Nadu" title="Tamil Nadu">Tamil Nadu</a> in <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a>. Other speakers of the language are recent immigrants from Tamil Nadu.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Chinese">Chinese</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=30" title="Edit section: Chinese">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Chinese_language" title="Chinese language">Chinese language</a></div>
<p>A majority of the people who immigrated in the 19th century were from <a href="/wiki/South_China" title="South China">southern China</a> and spoke the <a href="/wiki/Hakka_Chinese" title="Hakka Chinese">Hakka</a> and <a href="/wiki/Yue_Chinese" title="Yue Chinese">Yue</a> dialects of Chinese. In the 20th century after the years of indentureship up to the present-day more Chinese people have immigrated to Trinidad and Tobago for business and they speak the dialects of the indenturees along with other Chinese dialects, such as <a href="/wiki/Mandarin_Chinese" title="Mandarin Chinese">Mandarin</a> and <a href="/wiki/Min_Chinese" title="Min Chinese">Min</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-studycountry.com_130-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-studycountry.com-130">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-131" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-131">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> J. Dyer Ball, writing in 1906, says: "In Trinidad there were, about twenty years ago, 4,000 or 5,000 Chinese, but they have decreased to probably about 2,000 or 3,000, [2,200 in 1900]. They used to work in sugar plantations, but are now principally shopkeepers, as well as general merchants, miners and railway builders,
etc."<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Indigenous_languages">Indigenous languages</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=31" title="Edit section: Indigenous languages">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h4>
<p>The indigenous languages were Yao on Trinidad and <a href="/wiki/Karina_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Karina language">Karina</a> on Tobago, both Cariban, and <a href="/wiki/Shebaya_language" title="Shebaya language">Shebaya</a> on Trinidad, which was Arawakan.<sup id="cite_ref-studycountry.com_130-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-studycountry.com-130">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span><span class="mw-editsection"><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">[</span><a href="/w/index.php?title=Trinidad_and_Tobago&amp;action=edit&amp;section=32" title="Edit section: Religion">edit</a><span class="mw-editsection-bracket">]</span></span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Religion in Trinidad and Tobago">Religion in Trinidad and Tobago</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"/><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Further information: <a href="/wiki/Hinduism_in_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Hinduism in Trinidad and Tobago">Hinduism in Trinidad and Tobago</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sanatan_Dharma_Maha_Sabha" title="Sanatan Dharma Maha Sabha">Sanatan Dharma Maha Sabha</a>, <a href="/wiki/Arya_Samaj_in_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Arya Samaj in Trinidad and Tobago">Arya Samaj in Trinidad and Tobago</a>, <a href="/wiki/Islam_in_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Islam in Trinidad and Tobago">Islam in Trinidad and Tobago</a>, <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Catholic Church in Trinidad and Tobago">Catholic Church in Trinidad and Tobago</a>, <a href="/wiki/Diocese_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Diocese of Trinidad and Tobago">Diocese of Trinidad and Tobago</a>, <a href="/wiki/Presbyterian_Church_of_Trinidad_and_Tobago" title="Presbyterian Church of Trinidad and Tobago">Presbyterian Church of Trinidad and Tobago</a>, <a href="/wiki/Trinidad_Orisha" title="Trinidad Orisha">Trinidad Orisha</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Spiritual_Baptist" title="Spiritual Baptist">Spiritual Baptist</a></div>
<div class="thumb tright"><div class="thumbinner" style="width:172px;"><a href="/wiki/File:TnT_Hanuman_Statue_1.jpg" class="image"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/TnT_Hanuman_Statue_1.jpg/170px-TnT_Hanuman_Statue_1.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="227" class="thumbimage" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/TnT_Hanuman_Statue_1.jpg/255px-TnT_Hanuman_Statue_1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/2b/TnT_Hanuman_Statue_1.jpg/340px-TnT_Hanuman_Statue_1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1536" data-file-height="2048" /></a>  <div class="thumbcaption"><div class="magnify"><a href="/wiki/File:TnT_Hanuman_Statue_1.jpg" class="internal" title="Enlarge"></a></div>The 26-meter <a href="/wiki/Hanuman" title="Hanuman">Hanuman</a> murti in <a href="/wiki/Carapichaima" title="Carapichaima">Carapichaima</a>, a noted centre of Hindu and Indo-Trinidadian culture; it is the largest statue of Hanuman outside of <a href="/wiki/India" title="India">India</a></div></div></div>
<p>According to the 2011 census,<sup id="cite_ref-2011Census_3-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2011Census-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christianity</a> is the largest religion of the country, claimed as the faith of 63.2% of the population. <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholics" class="mw-redirect" title="Roman Catholics">Roman Catholics</a> were the largest single denomination, with 21.60% of the total population. The <a href="/wiki/Pentecostal" class="mw-redirect" title="Pentecostal">Pentecostal</a>/<a href="/wiki/Evangelical" class="mw-redirect" title="Evangelical">Evangelical</a>/<a href="/wiki/Full_Gospel" title="Full Gospel">Full Gospel</a> denominations were the third largest group with 12.02% of the population. Various other Christian denominations include (<a href="/wiki/Spiritual_Baptist" title="Spiritual Baptist">Spiritual Baptist</a> (5.67%), <a href="/wiki/Anglicans" class="mw-redirect" title="Anglicans">Anglicans</a> (5.67%), <a href="/wiki/Seventh-day_Adventists" class="mw-redirect" title="Seventh-day Adventists">Seventh-day Adventists</a> (4.09%), <a href="/wiki/Presbyterians" class="mw-redirect" title="Presbyterians">Presbyterians</a> or <a href="/wiki/Congregationalists" class="mw-redirect" title="Congregationalists">Congregationalists</a> (2.49%), <a href="/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witnesses" title="Jehovah&#39;s Witnesses">Jehovah's Witnesses</a> (1.47%), <a href="/wiki/Baptists" title="Baptists">Baptists</a> (1.21%), <a href="/wiki/Methodists" class="mw-redirect" title="Methodists">Methodists</a> (0.65%) and the <a href="/wiki/Moravian_Church" title="Moravian Church">Moravian Church</a> (0.27%)).
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Hinduism" title="Hinduism">Hinduism</a> was the second largest religion in the country, adhered to by 20.4% of the population in 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-2011Census_3-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2011Census-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> Hinduism is practised throughout the country, <a href="/wiki/Diwali" title="Diwali">Diwali</a> is a public holiday, and other <a href="/wiki/Hindu_holidays" class="mw-redirect" title="Hindu holidays">Hindu holidays</a> are also widely celebrated. The largest Hindu organization in Trinidad and Tobago is the <a href="/wiki/Sanatan_Dharma_Maha_Sabha" title="Sanatan Dharma Maha Sabha">Sanatan Dharma Maha Sabha</a>, which was formed in 1952 after the merging of the two main Hindu organizations. Most Hindus in Trinidad and Tobago are <a href="/wiki/San%C4%81tan%C4%AB" title="Sanātanī">Sanātanī (Sanatanist/Orthodox Hindu)</a>. Other sects and organizations include the <a href="/wiki/Arya_Samaj" title="Arya Samaj">Arya Samaj</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kabir_panth" title="Kabir panth">Kabir Panth</a>, Seunariani (Sieunarini/Siewnaraini/Shiv Narayani), <a href="/wiki/Ramanandi_Sampradaya" title="Ramanandi Sampradaya">Ramanandi Sampradaya</a>, <a href="/wiki/Aghor_Yoga" title="Aghor Yoga">Aughar (Aghor)</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kali" title="Kali">Kali Mai (Madrasi)</a>, <a href="/wiki/Sathya_Sai_Baba_movement" title="Sathya Sai Baba movement">Sathya Sai Baba movement</a>, <a href="/wiki/Shirdi_Sai_Baba_movement" title="Shirdi Sai Baba movement">Shirdi Sai Baba movement</a>, <a href="/wiki/International_Society_for_Krishna_Consciousness" title="International Society for Krishna Consciousness">ISKCON (Hare Krishna)</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chinmaya_Mission" title="Chinmaya Mission">Chinmaya Mission</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bharat_Sevashram_Sangha" title="Bharat Sevashram Sangha">Bharat Sevashram Sangha</a>, <a href="/wiki/Divine_Life_Society" title="Divine Life Society">Divine Life Society</a>, <a href="/wiki/Kaumaram" title="Kaumaram">Murugan (Kaumaram)</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ganapathi_Sachchidananda" title="Ganapathi Sachchidananda">Ganapathi Sachchidananda movement</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jagadguru_Kripalu_Parishat" title="Jagadguru Kripalu Parishat">Jagadguru Kripalu Parishat (Radha Madhav)</a> and <a href="/wiki/Brahma_Kumaris" title="Brahma Kumaris">Brahma Kumaris</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Muslim" class="mw-redirect" title="Muslim">Muslims</a> represented 4.97% of the population in 2011.<sup id="cite_ref-2011Census_3-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2011Census-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Eid_al-Fitr" title="Eid al-Fitr">Eid al-Fitr</a> is a public holiday and <a href="/wiki/Eid_al-Adha" title="Eid al-Adha">Eid al-Adha</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mawlid" title="Mawlid">Mawlid</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hosay" title="Hosay">Hosay</a>, <a href="/wiki/Shab-e-barat" class="mw-redirect" title="Shab-e-barat">Shab-e-barat</a>, and other <a href="/wiki/Islamic_holidays" title="Islamic holidays">Muslim holidays</a> are also celebrated.
</p><p>African-derived or Afrocentric religions are also practised, notably <a href="/wiki/Trinidad_Orisha" title="Trinidad Orisha">Trinidad Orisha</a> (<a href="/wiki/Yoruba_religion" title="Yoruba religion">Yoruba</a>) believers (0.9%) and <a href="/wiki/Rastafarians" class="mw-redirect" title="Rastafarians">Rastafarians</a> (0.27%).<sup id="cite_ref-2011Census_3-5" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-2011Census-3">&#91;3&#93;</a></sup> Various aspects of traditional <a href="/wiki/Obeah" title="Obeah">obeah</a> beliefs are still commonly practised on the islands.<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018251_38-4" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTERough_Guides2018251-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>There has been a Jewish community on the islands for many centuries, however their numbers have never been large, with a 2007 estimate putting the Jewish population at 55 individuals.<sup id="cite_ref-Luxner2007_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Luxner2007-135">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Siegel2015_136-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Siegel2015-136">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Respondents who did not state a religious affiliation represented 11.1% of the population, with 2.18% declaring themselves <a href="/wiki/Irreligious" class="mw-redirect" title="Irreligious">irreligious</a>.
</p>
<div class="barbox tright" style="overflow-x: auto;">

</div>
</div>
<p>Two <a href="/wiki/African_culture" class="mw-redirect" title="African culture">African</a> <a href="/wiki/Syncretic" class="mw-redirect" title="Syncretic">syncretic</a> faiths, the Shouter or <a href="/wiki/Spiritual_Baptist" title="Spiritual Baptist">Spiritual Baptists</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Trinidad_Orisha" title="Trinidad Orisha">Orisha</a> faith (formerly called <a href="/wiki/Shango" title="Shango">Shangos</a>, a less than complimentary term)<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (June 2016)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> are among the fastest growing religious groups. Similarly, there is a noticeable increase in numbers of <a href="/wiki/Evangelical_Protestant" class="mw-redirect" title="Evangelical Protestant">Evangelical Protestant</a> and <a href="/wiki/Fundamentalist_Christian" class="mw-redirect" title="Fundamentalist Christian">Fundamentalist</a> churches usually lumped as "<a href="/wiki/Pentecostal" class="mw-redirect" title="Pentecostal">Pentecostal</a>" by most Trinidadians, although this designation is often inaccurate. <a href="/wiki/Sikhism" title="Sikhism">Sikhism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Jainism" title="Jainism">Jainism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bah%C3%A1%CA%BC%C3%AD_Faith" title="Baháʼí Faith">Baháʼí</a>, <a href="/wiki/Zoroastrianism" title="Zoroastrianism">Zoroastrianism</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Buddhism" title="Buddhism">Buddhism</a> are practiced by a minority of <a href="/wiki/Indo-Trinidadian_and_Tobagonian" title="Indo-Trinidadian and Tobagonian">Indo-Trinidadian and Tobagonians</a>, mostly by recent immigrants from India. Several eastern religions such as <a href="/wiki/Buddhism" title="Buddhism">Buddhism</a>, <a href="/wiki/Chinese_folk_religion" title="Chinese folk religion">Chinese folk religion</a>, <a href="/wiki/Taoism" title="Taoism">Taoism</a> and <a href="/wiki/Confucianism" title="Confucianism">Confucianism</a> are followed by a minority of <a href="/wiki/Chinese_Trinidadian_and_Tobagonian" class="mw-redirect" title="Chinese Trinidadian and Tobagonian">Chinese Trinidadian and Tobagonian</a>, with most being Christians.
</p>
